id=\"offerings_update_form_3422_offerings_update_body\"\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eI thought it a good time to offer a mid-quarter update on the exciting progress we’re continuing to make at Reflex Protect and to express my gratitude to you all for your ongoing support and encouragement. We filed a prior update with Republic at the end of Q3, but it doesn’t appear to have been forwarded to you all via email, so this is another good opportunity to get you up to date.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eWe’re on schedule and on track for Q4 to represent our full and successful re-entry into an economy that shut us out (along with much of the rest of the world) back in March through an empowered launch of Reflex Protect Tactical into a marketplace in demand of the products and services we now offer.\u003cimg src=\"https://uploads.republic.co/p/images/attachments/original/000/029/540/29540-1606835617-aaf780a1d1156b0e6b7c2ee7613390e5d68babe4.png\" class=\"fr-fic fr-dib\"\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eHere are a few highlights of where we stand now:\u003c/p\u003e\u003cul\u003e\u003cli\u003eWe have one final contract left to sign with a top-flight sales agency for the Upper Midwest and Midwest territories, after which we will have \u003cstrong\u003ecomplete national coverage\u0026nbsp;\u003c/strong\u003evia seven experienced and effective representative organizations for law enforcement sales, from agencies and departments to dealers and distributors. These agreements represent no fewer than 40 new sales representatives covering the law enforcement market in all 50 states.\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eNotwithstanding the cancellation of SHOT Show, we still have plans for the Vegas Range Day we had scheduled during SHOT for attendance by our Western Regional sales agency and their top less-lethal customers, followed by taking that range demonstration around the country as “Road SHOT” to locations such as Fort Work, Miami-Dade, Cleveland, and Philadelphia to meet in-person with all our representative agencies and customers.\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eCovid-19 will dictate the timing for us to do this safely, but it has the potential to cost less and be far more effective than trying to compete with some 100,000 SHOT participants in January. While the big guys will go without SHOT entirely this year, we’ll actually create our own without competition from anyone.\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eWe have established a relationship with the largest EU distributor of less-lethal tech, located in the Netherlands. They have already made an initial order and are enthusiastic about the international potential for Presidia Gel and Reflex Remove alike. They are planning on attending our Vegas Range Day as well.\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eWhile Covid-19 has once again locked down the Middle East, we also have an agent there who expects a rescheduling of a recently canceled meeting with the Kuwait Army to demonstrate Reflex Remove – they also have tremendous enthusiasm for the potential there.\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eThe foregoing evidence the continued success of our hand-in-glove strategic alliance with the truly outstanding team at Melanie \u0026amp; Matt Shaefer’s Tactical Defense Training, LLC in Canton, OH. Cannot say enough about how our meeting at SHOT last January turned out to be a game-changer for all of us. Special thanks to them!\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eOver the course of the past month, we have published two scholarly, neutral White Papers supporting the implementation of our products and training.\u003cul\u003e\u003cli\u003eThe first, by Dr. Bayne French, explains for law enforcement medically, chemically, and practically why Presidia Gel is an entirely different and new controlling force less-lethal option, better than pepper spray or traditional CS gas and safer and more effective than Taser and other existing options.\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003e\u003ca href=\"https://reflexprotecttactical.com/wp-content/uploads/2020/11/RPT_LE_WhitePaper_2020.pdf\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003eTactical Medical White Paper\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/li\u003e\u003c/ul\u003e\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eThe Healthcare White Paper is intended to provide nurses’ unions, caregivers, security, and others advocating for safer healthcare workplace conditions and violence prevention training and equipment with compelling efficacy, safety, and most importantly, economic arguments for adopting our Active Defense Solution.\u003cul\u003e\u003cli\u003e\u003ca href=\"https://cdn.shopify.com/s/files/1/2359/3295/files/RP_HEALTHCARE_White_Paper_2020.pdf?v=1604481576\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003eHealthcare White Paper\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003e\u003ca href=\"https://cdn.shopify.com/s/files/1/2359/3295/files/Healthcare_Supervisor_Summary.pdf?v=1606809483\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003eHealthcare Supervisor Summary\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/li\u003e\u003c/ul\u003e\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eThis latter White Paper and other new marketing materials aimed specifically at COVID-SAFE training options is being distributed widely by our healthcare group purchasing organization partner HRS, with whom we were hardly underway last January when the pandemic shut us down. Having this 30-strong sales team now able for the first time in months to focus their customers on something other than PPE is a key opportunity for which we are well prepared.\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eWe have completed our new clamshell packaging for consumer retail sales and are presently setting up distribution opportunities online and in brick \u0026amp; mortar locations such as Dick’s Sports, Big 5, and a wide range of other retailers for Q1.\u003cbr\u003e\u003cimg src=\"https://uploads.republic.co/p/images/attachments/original/000/029/543/29543-1606835806-8c5e83ca400983890a682ace0a726dec42e4e26b.png\" class=\"fr-fic fr-dib\"\u003eWe are also completing the R\u0026amp;D process for a purely consumer-oriented product (i.e. a keychain or clip-on Presidia Gel for joggers, hikers, etc.) specifically for this market.\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eWe are presently in advanced discussions with one of our existing strategic alliances to piggy-back on this entry into consumer retail and advancing our reputation and presence in the LE market will be considerably expanded by way of the release of the “Street Ready” less-lethal bundle (holster, 1.9 oz PG, aerosol RR) for patrol officers and consumers who want to “Use What the Heroes Use” everywhere.\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eWe are catching the attention of the national press more frequently, and although we still haven’t had the breakout coverage of an incident (most of which are understandably kept secret), coverage of national conversations around police shootings, use of force, workplace violence, an armed and divided citizenry, and civil unrest without mention of the existence and availability of an all-new less-lethal option responsive to the needs of the day is becoming untenable. Several journalists have suggested they expect our story to hit the news when the present election cycle slows a bit and during a time when good news might be welcomed.\u003c/li\u003e\u003c/ul\u003e\u003cp\u003eWe wish you all a healthy and safe holiday season. id=\"offerings_update_form_2691_offerings_update_body\"\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eThanks to all of you that have followed Reflex Protect over the course of this campaign. It has been an amazing four months. \u0026nbsp;There would have been no way I could have predicted at launch that our immediate pivot from what we were designed to do into what it turns out we are meant to do would happen so rapidly. Our mission to alleviate unnecessary suffering from violence for everyone, everywhere, is making incredible strides as we speak -- law enforcement across the country is learning through our new distribution deals and sales agents that there is now available a replacement for the pepper spray found on nearly every duty belt in the country, nearly universally despised by the officers that carry it, and that the replacement is not only better (Target Specific/Fast Acting/Rapidly Reversible) it's also more humane for everyone involved. It's a serious win-win during a time when we could really use one in this country.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eThis fact has not been lost on our competitors and/or tangential players in the market either. \u0026nbsp;The rapid expansion of our distribution capacity and ability to sell to law enforcement and corrections facilities across the nation in August, combined with a couple exciting international opportunities moving rapidly as well, has been noticed by everyone. \u0026nbsp;While you all know the limitations of what can and can't be said on this site, I do feel confident and comfortable stating that Reflex Protect is now the subject of M\u0026amp;A discussions at the C-Suite level of more than one publicly traded company involved \u0026nbsp;in the law enforcement and/or outdoor recreational markets. \u0026nbsp;We are grateful to the attention this Republic campaign helped bring to our company and specifically to the weekly updates over the past months that have shown our rise in real time.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eThanks again to our investors, followers, and to Republic for this exciting campaign. \u0026nbsp;We hope you all keep following us, because we'll keep making news and waves. \u0026nbsp;All best from the Reflex Protect team!\u003c/p\u003e\u003c/textarea\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cdiv class='s-marginTop1_5 s-marginPullHoriz1 one of our first Reflex Protect sessions, carrying a pocket Presidia Gel on his duty belt, chose to use it when responding to a potentially violent domestic dispute on the 4\u003csup\u003eth\u003c/sup\u003e of July. \u0026nbsp;Faced with a size and strength disadvantage of an inebriated man, who ignored repeated verbal commands, the officer unholstered his Reflex Protect. \u0026nbsp;Even though the man lowered his sunglasses to shield his eyes, a single 2-second blast from six feet away rendered him immediately unable to see and disoriented.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eAfter backup officers brought him under full control and placed him in handcuffs, the man was rapidly cleaned up with Reflex Remove. \u0026nbsp;The other officers, not yet been trained on Presidia Gel, were so impressed by the speed of both it and Reflex Remove, they reached out to us to ask if they could purchase both using \u003cem\u003etheir own personal uniform allowance\u003c/em\u003e, rather than waiting for the department to complete a full product vetting process.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eWhat we have learned is that police are far more likely to use Presidia Gel – if verbal de-escalation has not succeeded – than any other less-than-lethal method presently available to them. \u0026nbsp;The result is that far fewer such encounters should result in injury or lasting harm to anyone.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e“The last time I deployed a chemical agent aerosol was OC spray and there was significant cross-contamination that day; but not enough to incapacitate myself, my chief or the fire department personnel on scene. It was a relief being able to deploy a chemical agent that didn't significantly affect everyone else. I am very pleased the only person disoriented by the Presidia Gel was the subject. All officers on scene were able to return to full duty immediately following the call.”\u003c/p\u003e\u003cul\u003e\u003cli\u003e– Officer Marchionda, SPD, Northeast Ohio\u003c/li\u003e\u003c/ul\u003e\u003cp\u003eBeing able to resolve – safely and quickly – this potentially violent situation speaks to the potential for how Presidia Gel can immediately impact law banking, for Union Bank in California and then in relationship banking for Capital One Bank in New York. \u0026nbsp;When I came back to finish school at the University of Montana, I studied business management focusing on entrepreneurship. While in school, I acquired my insurance license and traveled the state selling health insurance. This allowed me to work closely with an overwhelmed elderly population, which really awakened my sense of purpose through service. \u0026nbsp;After several years of success in the insurance business, I moved back East, this time Boston. In Boston I sold residential and commercial energy for NRG and attended as many Red Sox, Celtics, and Bruins games as possible. Despite my love for new England and all that it had to offer, I felt as though it could never compare to the family I missed, and the beauty I experienced growing up in Montana.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eOnce settled back in my home state, I met Ben Gladwin and Joe Anderson. It quickly became apparent that they both shared a genuine desire to be of service. President Just Spoke About the Need for New Less-Lethal Technology\u003c/h3\u003e\u003cp\u003eJust this morning in a White House Rose Garden Press Conference announcing a new Executive Order, President Donald Trump spoke of the police reform measures his administration is advancing.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e“We are looking at new advanced and powerful less-lethal weapons to help prevent deadly interactions,” explained President Trump. \u0026nbsp;“New devices are being developed all the time, and we’re looking at the best of them. And cost is no object.”\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eWe welcome this focus from the White House and highest levels of the Justice Department, but we’re already responding to this need at the local level. As soon as hospitals and school districts across the country began considering Reflex Protect®, they turned to local law enforcement for advice.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eNot only did those departments whole-heartedly support its use as a “response gap” measure, they also became intrigued by our innovations – the Reflex technology spray head shooting a “bulls-eye” stream of Presidia Gel® that took down an assailant faster than pepper spray and could then be cleaned up faster than any of them had experienced. \u0026nbsp;Some departments bought healthcare versions for themselves immediately.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eOver time, however, law enforcement’s demand for a new controlling force tool designed to prevent physical contact and any sort of lasting harm became more insistent, and Covid-19 made it immediate. At the onset of the pandemic, we shifted focus to this new demand from law enforcement. As you’ll see from the live video and photos below from recent field tests by our strategic partner Tactical Defense Training (TDT) in Canton, Ohio, our rapid onset, non-atomizing Presidia Gel is exactly what they needed, and Reflex Remove makes it even more attractive given increased focus on limiting use of force injuries for all involved.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e“Skepticism is a survival mechanism,” explains TDT president Matt Schaefer, a former police officer and U.S. Army veteran, with experience in corrections, uniform patrol, warrants, narcotics, investigations and SWAT since 1995.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eHe knows that for officers at all levels, seeing is believing. \u0026nbsp;That is why Schaefer has been running trainings with officers to show them up close how both Presidia Gel® and Reflex Remove™ work faster and effectively than anything else they have ever seen.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e“All the reasons cops don’t like using OC [pepper] spray simply do not exist with Reflex Protect,” explains Schaefer. \u0026nbsp;“It is truly a game changer for Law Enforcement.”\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eSee for yourself how officers respond to Presidia Gel and Reflex Remove for the first time. Reflex Protect has the potential to impact not only conversations about violence in the U.S., but also the immediate and important issues raised by recent demonstrations.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan class=\"fr-video fr-fvc fr-dvb fr-draggable\"\u003e\u003ciframe src=\"https://www.youtube.com/embed/OaP87s82emE\" frameborder=\"0\" fr-dib\"\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eBeing self-reliant is a cornerstone of the Reflex Protect® mantra, and we practice what we preach in all aspects of our business.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eReflex Protect®, Presidia Gel®, and Reflex Remove™ are 100% American made. \u0026nbsp;Our patented spray head was invented by Montanan Steve Mangold with design work by Salient Technologies in Bozeman. The injection-mold plastics are manufactured by PDM in Manhattan, Montana, next door to where Blackhawk produces our custom holsters for security and law enforcement.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eOur patent-pending formula for Reflex Remove was invented in the University of Montana campus tech incubator by Missoula-based chemist and Reflex team member Patrick Memoli. It is presently the subject of a National Science Foundation Small Business Innovation Grant Phase 2 research application due to its potential to eliminate unnecessary suffering.\u003cbr\u003e\u003cbr\u003eAnd when it’s time to assemble new product, as we are doing now due to increased demand, we do it at Big Sky Fulfillment in Missoula. \u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eThe mechanical star of our line is a $30,000 Schmidt industrial press, also designed by Steve, that attaches the bottom enclosure onto the pressurized can of Presidia Gel.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eThe Reflex spray head then goes through some delicate hand assembly of other pieces, followed by testing, cleaning and packaging (all done with healthy social distancing in mind).\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eWith the problems of supply chains from overseas impacting American capacity for manufacturing, it’s good to know that Reflex Protect can take care of our customers by ourselves.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e---\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003ca href=\"https://reflex-protect.ck.page/ebook\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003eDownload\u003c/a\u003e next story is from our teacher.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eMeet Libby Brunell\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eI have lived most of my life in Montana. \u0026nbsp;When I graduated college and began my career as a classroom teacher, I never thought that a conversation about guns in schools would impact my life. \u0026nbsp;Then Columbine, Sandy Hook, Parkland and multiple other school shootings happened and unfortunately, it became part of our daily routines in schools, with active shooter trainings and lockdown drills and evacuation plans. \u0026nbsp;My two kiddos are in 3\u003csup\u003erd\u003c/sup\u003e and 5\u003csup\u003eth\u003c/sup\u003e grade, and this new reality scares me.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eThen I met Joe Anderson, the CEO of Reflex Protect® and learned how the company was revolutionizing non-lethal defense. \u0026nbsp;As a former educator, I was particularly impressed and excited about the training program, which teaches teachers, workers, nurses, and more how to diffuse difficult, potentially violent situations and how to be their own first responder in the gap between an event happening and the professionals (law enforcement, EMS, etc.) arriving.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eBeing COO of Reflex Protect® is an inspiring and humbling journey. \u0026nbsp;The hope this company brings to a world darkened by violence makes me grateful every day. \u0026nbsp;I’m thankful to work with a team that empowers people to keep themselves and their loved ones safe and helps the world be a little safer for all of us.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eAlso,\u0026nbsp;\u003ca href=\"https://reflex-protect.ck.page/ebook\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003elink\u003c/a\u003e to our free Reflex Protect® fr-dib\"\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eWhat inspired you to found Reflex Protect®?\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eI grew up on a cattle ranch my great-grandfather homesteaded on the Alberta, Canada border of Montana before it was a state. It’s one of the most remote parts of the lower-48, and I graduated from a public high school with just 48 others students. I was always a little different, I suppose, evidenced by me becoming the world-wide President of Key Club International in 1982-83, despite coming from such an out-of-the-way place.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eI went on to graduate from Stanford University, majoring in Political Science with honors in the Strategic Weapons Management track (supervised by such luminaries as future Secretary of State Condaleeza Rice), after which I received a JD from Georgetown. I returned to San Francisco to work at Pettit \u0026amp; Martin, but after the 101 California Shooting, I changed direction completely, first joining a rock band, August West, and then starting my own entertainment law firm and becoming the Professor of Entertainment Law at the dawn of the digital age.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eMy entrepreneurial career began in response to my personal trauma losing colleagues and friends in the 1993 101 California Shooting in San Francisco. When my co-founder, inventor Steve Mangold, placed a prototype of what would become Reflex Protect in my hands, suggesting that he needed help in developing it as an alternative to a loaded gun in a nightstand drawer, I knew instantly I was looking at the next step of my entrepreneurial journey.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003ca href=\"https://republic.co/blog/joe-anderson-reflex-protect\" id=\"offerings_update_form_2284_offerings_update_body\"\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cimg src=\"https://uploads.republic.co/p/images/attachments/original/000/020/805/20805-1590782090-f71adf1ded0333987d6a599c9fb89cfc66918fa0.jpeg\" class=\"fr-fic fr-dib\"\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eOur black belt instructor tells the fourth story.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eMeet Alain Burrese.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eI’ve been fascinated with Asia and martial arts since I read Fred Neff and Bruce Tegner books in the 1970s and practiced moves in my living room. I’ve been at it ever since, including living and training in South Korea while studying Hapkido, in which I hold a 5\u003csup\u003eth\u003c/sup\u003e degree black belt. \u0026nbsp;My first book on self-defense was published by Paladin Press in 1996, and since then I’ve put out 11 instructional DVDS and eight more books. I’ve always loved teaching, both Hapkido as well as safety and self-defense programs for people who didn’t want to learn a traditional martial art.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eAlthough I returned from Korea to attend law school and practiced law for a few years, I felt the most fulfilled writing, speaking, and teaching on the topics of martial arts, safety, and self-defense.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eThen in 2012, the shooting at the Aurora, Colorado, movie theater occurred. My students and friends started asking what to do in these situations, so I added Active Shooter Response to my teaching. \u0026nbsp;As shootings around the country continued, I ultimately wrote the book \u003cem\u003eSurvive a Shooting: Strategies to Survive Active Shooters and Terrorist Attacks\u003c/em\u003e. Through it all, my philosophy has always been to help people stay safe, not to be paranoid. I want to help people enjoy life, safely.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eWhen I met Joe Anderson in 2018, and learned about Reflex Protect, my immediate thought was: “This is the missing piece!” \u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eI’d been teaching people how to defend themselves with improvised weapons simply because my school and hospital clients didn’t allow firearms, and pepper spray couldn’t be used inside without affecting innocent bystanders and having the entire building evacuated and shut down. \u0026nbsp;But Reflex Protect brilliantly solves these issues. \u0026nbsp;It is a unique non-lethal self-defense product – the best I’ve seen during decades in this field.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eReflex Protect’s mission and philosophy align with what I’ve been teaching for years, and I’m proud to be the Director of Active Defense Training for the company. \u0026nbsp;It’s truly rewarding to work with such a committed and dedicated team helping empower people with the knowledge and tools to keep themselves and those they care about safe.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eAlso,\u0026nbsp;\u003ca href=\"https://reflex-protect.ck.page/ebook\" target=\"_blank\" id=\"offerings_update_form_2255_offerings_update_body\"\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cimg src=\"https://uploads.republic.co/p/images/attachments/original/000/020/597/20597-1590167445-81cec6a0df491571bfdc0fb5b0a6cf53c6c364d0.jpg\" class=\"fr-fic fr-dib\"\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eIf you had told me in art school that the self-defense industry would play such a huge role in my career and life, I would have laughed out loud. But here I am, with close to a decade in the self-defense industry, and not only am I grateful to be working in it, but I also love it.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eI’m an alumnus of the Cleveland Institute of Art, where I studied design, sculpture and glass. My initial dreams were to see my car designs driving down the main roads of the USA. But after a year studying industrial design, I realized I wanted to use my creative powers for good. \u0026nbsp; As an early example, my senior thesis was on shark conservation, and I have created artwork to educate and raise awareness on the importance and conservation of sharks from that time up until today, in addition to being a graphic designer.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eThings turned to self-defense when Mace Brand® moved to Cleveland and was looking for a Creative Manager. \u0026nbsp;I thought, “What a cool job! \u0026nbsp;I can use my creative talents to help individuals be safe.” I ended up running the marketing and design department for the world-renowned brand for six years. \u0026nbsp;That time revealed my passion for self-defense. As a creative person, I felt good about the work I was doing and helping the world on so many levels.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eAt the end of 2018, I felt I was ready for the next step in my career goals and dreams and I resigned from Mace to start my own creative agency, DesignPod Studio. \u0026nbsp;In doing so, word spread, and within time I was connected with Joe Anderson, CEO of Reflex Protect®. Right away it became apparent how perfect this partnership could and would become. I didn’t realize how much I had missed working in the self-defense sector, not to mention how my experience and talent could be an asset and propellant for Reflex Protect®.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eI feel grateful, excited, and inspired to be working with the newest and best self-defense solution! As a creative, I feel truly fulfilled as my work is making a difference in the world. method=\"post\"\u003e\u003cinput name=\"utf8\" type=\"hidden\" value=\"\u0026#x2713;\" autocomplete=\"off\" /\u003e\u003cinput type=\"hidden\" name=\"_method\" value=\"patch\" autocomplete=\"off\" healthcare safety in the age of Covid-19.\" name=\"offerings_update[headline]\" id=\"offerings_update_form_2240_offerings_update_headline\" /\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cdiv class='js-headline_error u-colorRed s-marginPullTop0_5 s-marginBottom1 s-marginLeft0_5 u-hidden'\u003e\nEntry must be under 140 characters.\n\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cdiv class='offerings-updates-shared-form__body-wrapper'\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"c-input-group\"\u003e\u003ctextarea class=\"c-input--textarea c-input c-input--required js-offering_update_body\" required=\"required\" data-show-video-autoplay-button=\"false\" data-file-formats=\"application/msword application/vnd.openxmlformats-officedocument.wordprocessingml.document application/pdf application/vnd.openxmlformats-officedocument.spreadsheetml.sheet application/vnd.openxmlformats-officedocument.presentationml.presentation\" aria-required=\"true\" placeholder=\"Description\" name=\"offerings_update[body]\" id=\"offerings_update_form_2240_offerings_update_body\"\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cimg src=\"https://uploads.republic.co/p/images/attachments/original/000/020/364/20364-1589834686-f8bd39a55db6c332c123ce4b9673c36077dbf650.jpg\" class=\"fr-fic fr-dib\"\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eIf the nation has learned anything from the Covid-19 crisis, it’s that advance planning in safety preparedness is vital to our ability to respond to emergencies, whether they’re viral, violent, or otherwise. Healthcare organizations in particular need procedures and sufficient up-to-date equipment to quickly respond to potentially confusing and fluid circumstances.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eThe coronavirus crisis has also illuminated the dangerous problem of workplace violence against medical caregivers, an issue that healthcare organizations now have a unique opportunity to address.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003e\u003cu\u003e7 out of 10\u0026nbsp;\u003c/u\u003e\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eOf all professions, healthcare workers are the number one victims of workplace violence across the country. OSHA reports that 70% of claims for injuries from violence occur in a healthcare setting. Until relatively recently, many nursing veterans (and the administrators responsible for their health and well-being) considered the occasional injury caused by an act of violence in a hospital “a part of the job.” \u0026nbsp;Because all medical professionals want their facilities to be seen as safe places of healing, these disturbing stories often stayed unknown.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eThere is movement to change this. \u0026nbsp;Across the country new legislation is making battery of a caregiver a felony regardless if any injury occurred. This is similar to how assaults on law enforcement and the judiciary are treated.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eAlthough laudable for bringing the problem into the light, we don’t need more felonies so much as we need fewer batteries. Preparations designed to deter, prevent, or stop an assault before it ever becomes a potentially injury-causing battery truly is the goal.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003e\u003cu\u003ePrepare + Train\u003c/u\u003e\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eHealthcare workers receive little and often zero training on how to recognize and respond to violence in their workplace, but they should. Imagine instead if this training was part of their licensing curriculum prior to working in acute/hospital settings and mental health treatment locations. Discussions about planning and preparation could begin well before and segue into training in other facility policies and procedures.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eFor those who believe anti-violence safety preparedness should be left to “security personnel,” there are two very important reasons not to:\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e1) \u0026nbsp;The response gap between the time when potential violence erupts, and help arrives is often filled with terror and injury. Trained security cannot simply be everywhere instantly.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e2) \u0026nbsp;Many smaller or rural facilities have no onsite security at all. Local law enforcement is the closest help.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eThe April 2018 \u003cem\u003eSentinel Alert\u003c/em\u003e, published by the Joint Commission healthcare accreditation organization, declared that healthcare facilities must provide adequate training in response codes and anti-violence safety preparation to all healthcare workers, including but not limited to security. Few hospitals or healthcare organizations have yet taken this admonition fully to heart by establishing and integrating well thought out anti-violence safety preparedness policies, procedures, tools, and training.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003e\u003cu\u003eYour Own First Responder\u003c/u\u003e\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003ePost-Covid implementation of new and better pandemic response procedures, as well as newfound public appreciation for the role caregivers play (and the risks they take), provides a perfect opportunity for healthcare organizations to address workplace violence safety preparedness head-on.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eWhile healthcare workers should understand that suffering injury due to violence is absolutely not “part of the job,” equally important is training to understand another simple truth: “You are your own first responder.” Not security, not your panic alarm, not law enforcement – you. And every second counts. Seconds count in the case of an active shooter, for example, because one person is shot every 15 seconds \u003cem\u003eon average\u003c/em\u003e in such an incident (more in any given burst). Medical workers need to know how to react immediately to the threat of violence, regardless of its source.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003e\u003cu\u003eCompassionate Controlling Force\u003c/u\u003e\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eHealthcare workers should receive at least annual training in verbal and non-verbal de-escalation techniques, reasonable physical intervention methods, and the basics of “Run/Hide/Fight” and how it may or may not apply to a caregiver with a duty of care to her patients. Because of this special duty, however, caregivers (and security alike) should also have access to and training in defensive tools that offer compassionate controlling force.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eIn the past, this was called planned improvisation and you were taught using improvised weapons. This type of instruction, ironically, itself often led to unnecessary trauma or injury. The best practice today is “low impact, no scare tactics” training using modern defensive chemical formulations specially designed for use inside sensitive environments (i.e. not pepper sprays), accompanied by fast-acting decontamination solutions.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eTraining in the use of and access to such tools is also not just for security, but should support registration clerks, orderlies, and bedside caregivers alike. Everyone should be trained to act as his or her own first responder – the “response gap” allows no time to wait for help.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003e\u003cu\u003eStep Back\u003c/u\u003e\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eAnother simple thing is training to protect ones’ personal space and reactionary distance. According to industry-standard MOAB training, the brain takes 1.5 seconds to recognize a threat, form a response, and physically respond. \u0026nbsp;That’s why caregivers need to step back a minimum of four feet so an aggressive person can’t touch them and they have the ability to deflect and move if the assailant throws something or advances in a threatening manner.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eInterestingly, this last point ties in directly to lessons learned from Covid-19. \u0026nbsp;Healthcare workers are trained to move toward emergency situations. Over the past weeks, many nurses have had to relearn this training, remaining mindful to stop and first put on PPE before entering a Covid-19 patient’s room to protect themselves and their colleagues from infection. \u0026nbsp;Likewise, in case of potential violence, caregivers require training in the counter-intuitive need to stop, back up, and create space so they can physically respond to the situation, including accessing and deploying a compassionate and controlling tool if necessary.\u003cbr\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003e\u003cu\u003eWorking The Pause\u003c/u\u003e\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eWhile so many communities are on lockdown, healthcare facilities have a unique opportunity to start implementing much needed policies, procedures, and training in the use of modern best practice anti-violence safety preparedness solutions, alongside the newly apparent need for focused pandemic response solutions. Post-Covid attention to physical design or redesign of spaces, including barriers, escape routes, and visitor management systems should be on the agenda to protect caregivers as well.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eSafety preparedness is always something easily left for another time. The lessons of Covid-19, however, make clear that the only good time to prepare for an emergency in the future is today.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp style=\"text-align: center;\"\u003e\u003cem\u003eShawn Paul is the Director of Enterprise Physical Security,\u003c/em\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\u003cem\u003eSafety \u0026amp; Emergency Mgmt. Risk \u0026amp; Integrity Services at\u003c/em\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\u003cem\u003eProvidence St. Joseph’s Healthcare\u003c/em\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp style=\"text-align: center;\"\u003e\u003cem\u003eJoe Anderson is the CEO of Reflex Protect,\u0026nbsp;\u003c/em\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\u003cem\u003emaker of America’s only “hospital safe”\u003c/em\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\u003cem\u003ecompassionate active defense solution\u003c/em\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cem\u003e\u003cimg src=\"https://uploads.republic.co/p/images/attachments/original/000/020/365/20365-1589834714-211405af08d3ac98c6c99c7d1187389a31e06355.png\" class=\"fr-fic fr-dib\" style=\"width: 219px;\"\u003e\u003c/em\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003c/textarea\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cdiv class='s-marginTop1_5 id=\"reflex-protect-team-spotlight-p-img-src-https-uploads-republic-co-p-images\" class=\"offerings-updates-shared-card\" data-rc=\"offerings/updates/shared/card\"\u003e\u003cdiv data-slug='reflex-protect-jessica-adanich-reflex-protect-team-spotlight-our-police-officer'\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cform class=\"simple_form js-offerings_update_form\" id=\"offerings_update_form_2228_edit_offerings_update_2228\" action=\"/offerings/updates/reflex-protect-jessica-adanich-reflex-protect-team-spotlight-our-police-officer\" accept-charset=\"UTF-8\" id=\"offerings_update_form_2228_offerings_update_body\"\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cimg src=\"https://uploads.republic.co/p/images/attachments/original/000/020/281/20281-1589563305-6bc9432f555a01ff6ad6ace99d6b1b7b71d95d11.jpg\" class=\"fr-fic fr-dib\"\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eOur police officer tells the second story.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eMeet Ben Gladwin.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eAs Captain at the University of Montana police force, one of my favorite responsibilities was leading a program called \u003cem\u003eImmediate Response to an Armed Intruder\u003c/em\u003e. We taught university staff and faculty and local public school teachers and healthcare workers how to survive the response gap between a violent incident starting and the arrival of help from someone like me. \u0026nbsp;Our model was Escape, Deny, Defend (run, hide, fight). \u0026nbsp;Now these people did not sign up for workplace violence, and I could tell the “defend” part was intimidating and frightening for them to even think about. \u0026nbsp;As a police officer, I had gear, training, experience, and often back-up to deal with violence. \u0026nbsp;These people had none of that. \u0026nbsp;Truth be told, it never sat well with me that the best practice was to teach them to use improvised weapons (throw a chair or stapler) at an active shooter.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eWhen I was introduced to Reflex Protect, I imagined pepper spray. \u0026nbsp;My experience was that pepper spray never worked as intended, typically had a delayed onset, and contaminated everyone and everything nearby. \u0026nbsp;I’ve been hit with pepper spray – as was every cadet at the police academy – and I fought through it while completing several tasks before the effects became debilitating.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eWhat I liked immediately was how their spray head could easily be grabbed and targeted accurately when an adrenaline dump causes the loss of fine motor skills. \u0026nbsp;From teaching civilians, I knew this was an ongoing issue. \u0026nbsp;I also liked that the product came out in a tight stream that did not atomize, did not contain OC, and therefore could be used indoors (which I knew was a non-starter for pepper spray).\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eOf course, the skeptical cop in me need to see the evidence. So I asked to experience Presidia® gel firsthand. \u0026nbsp;I absolutely expected to fight through it – as I could with pepper spray – but the pain was instant, severe, and debilitating. \u0026nbsp;I could \u003cem\u003enot\u003c/em\u003e open my eyes. \u0026nbsp;It felt like white-hot needles going straight to the back of my eyeballs. \u0026nbsp;All my thoughts were on making it stop. \u0026nbsp;As thoroughly impressed as I was with effectiveness of Presidia gel, in that moment I was eternally grateful for the antidote, Reflex Remove. Within three minutes I could open my eyes and function. \u0026nbsp;Other than a mild burning sensation on my skin, I had completely recovered in 15. \u0026nbsp;If it had been pepper spray, everyone in the room would have been affected, the whole building would have been cleared and I would have been dealing with the effects for the rest of the day.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eThe whole experience, ironically, left me in awe.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eThis was a game changer for me both as an officer and as a trainer of teachers and nurses. I immediately wanted it in my daughter’s classroom, as did she. \u0026nbsp;I became an advisor for the company and realized I was following a passion – helping people plan, prepare, and train for surviving the unthinkable. I quickly committed to full time, knowing that I could save more lives here than sitting behind a desk the police department.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eI love giving our customers peace of mind, empowerment, and confidence in knowing that Reflex Protect is next to them in their workplace. I’m personally confident that Reflex Protect is the missing piece to the last line of defense for the true first responders: the frontline people who are present when violence erupts.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003ca href=\"https://reflex-protect.ck.page/ebook\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003eLink\u003c/a\u003e to our free Reflex Protect® eBook providing you with important safety tips useful wherever you live, work, worship, or play.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cspan class=\"fr-video fr-fvc fr-dvb fr-draggable\"\u003e\u003ciframe src=\"https://www.youtube.com/embed/6BoHHpituPo\" frameborder=\"0\" c-input--optional\" name=\"offerings_update[visible_to]\" id=\"offerings_update_form_2228_offerings_update_visible_to\"\u003e\u003coption selected=\"selected\" href=\"javascript:void(0)\"\u003eCancel\u003c/a\u003e\n\u003cinput type=\"submit\" name=\"commit\" value=\"Save changes\" class=\"c-button js-offerings_update_form_submit\" data-disable-with=\"Save changes\" /\u003e\n\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003c/form\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cdiv class='js-offerings_update_content_wrapper'\u003e\n\u003cdiv class='u-textRight s-paddingBottom0_5 offerings-updates-shared-card__date-wrapper'\u003e\n\u003cdiv class='s-fontSize25 u-fontWeight400 offerings-updates-shared-card__date-day-month'\u003e\nMay 15\n\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cdiv class='s-fontSize13 u-colorGrayA offerings-updates-shared-card__date-year'\u003e\n2020\n\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"offerings-updates-shared-card_content\" data-rc=\"offerings/updates/shared/card_content\"\u003e\u003cdiv class='s-paddingTop2 s-paddingHoriz2 offerings-updates-shared-card_content__content-wrapper'\u003e\n\u003ch2 class='c-condensedTitle s-fontSize35 s-marginBottom1_5 offerings-updates-shared-card_content__title'\u003e\n\u003ca fr-dib\"\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eReflex Protect proudly announces a fully online distance learning Reflex Protect training option. This offers a turn-key solution to certified instruction requirements when Reflex Protect in-person training is not feasible or preferred.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eWe brought this enhanced capacity online months ahead of schedule due to Covid-19 lockdowns and the need to still deliver in-house “Train the Trainer” certification of our non-lethal active defense solution without our team ever setting foot in the customer’s facility. It also allows for more frequent follow-up training for facilities we do visit in person.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eAs an example, our team was scheduled to conduct an in-house training this week at Billings Clinic, Montana’s largest hospital. Covid-19 safety considerations forced its cancellation. Reacting quickly to the new reality, Billings Clinic will instead receive online instruction and certification for two of its security leaders who will then each train about 30 more staff in the use of Reflex Protect. User certification for those users will also be completed through Reflex Protect training.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eBy June 1st, any hospital or school in the country (and any business by mid-summer) can go online and for $299.95 download a complete training package that includes:\u003c/p\u003e\u003cul\u003e\u003cli\u003eForm policies and procedures for the institution or its board to customize and implement\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eFAQ video explaining the purpose and reasoning behind the course and materials\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eTrainer certification course and printable certificate for local responsible security personnel\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eCourse materials (printed or digital handouts, PowerPoint, videos, etc.) for use by Certified Trainer in training users\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eSample full training video for relevant facility (i.e. hospital or school)\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eSupplemental materials\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eUser certification video and printable certifications for all trained users\u003c/li\u003e\u003c/ul\u003e\u003cp\u003eUnlike most training courses, we teach local security leaders in the why, how, when, and when not to use Reflex Protect Active Defense Solution as part of their safety preparedness program. \u0026nbsp;This certifies for workers’ comp, general liability, and community approval that everyone authorized to use Reflex Protect has been properly trained in its use and in some level of general anti-violence safety preparedness as well.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eA full active shooter training is also in the works. \u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003ca href=\"https://reflex-protect.ck.page/ebook\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003eLink\u003c/a\u003e to our free Reflex id=\"offerings_update_form_2199_offerings_update_body\"\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eThe secret sauce of Reflex Protect® is not limited to our innovative technology, intellectual property, and comprehensive solution to a major problem, as important as those are. What makes Reflex Protect® so special is the quality and comprehensive expertise of the people drawn to its mission. Please get to know our team better through these short first-person accounts. Feel free to comment or ask questions of any team member!\u003cbr\u003e\u003cbr\u003eOur storyteller tells the first story.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cimg src=\"https://uploads.republic.co/p/images/attachments/original/000/020/055/20055-1588972804-b44c3b58f9b333b6ed691a869656dab348f2e60a.png\" class=\"fr-fic fr-dib\"\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eMeet Sue Glader.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eI am a pacifist. The closest to conflict I’ve ever gotten was purposefully stepping on the lunch sack (with both feet) of a little girl in elementary school who was being particularly nasty to me at recess.\u003cbr\u003e\u003cbr\u003eI am also risk adverse. My natural inclination is to stay in my lane and keep my head down.\u003cbr\u003e\u003cbr\u003eSo when I first heard about Reflex Protect two years ago, as Joe is an old friend, I dismissed it immediately. I didn’t want to think about needing protection, because I didn’t want to think about anything bad happening to me.\u003cbr\u003e\u003cbr\u003eAnd then we had out of control fires in Northern California that literally obliterated whole cities. Our air turned brown. We all packed “to go” bags left by the front door hastily stuffed with photocopies of important documents, spare contact lenses, and a change of clothes. I actually put my most precious photo albums in the trunk of my car, just in case.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eAnd then we had mandatory power outages in Northern California that lasted for a week. And we had to find stores with generators who still had bags of ice for our coolers, batteries for lanterns and flashlights, and back-up chargers for our electronics. We left our garage doors open with our cars facing out.\u003cbr\u003eIn short, I came to understand the wisdom of preparation.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eIt is why I am now part of the Reflex Protect team, helping to tell the story of why preparation with non-lethal protection is at the heart of peace of mind. As a peaceful person, that’s all I want for everyone.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e- Sue Glader\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003ca href=\"https://reflex-protect.ck.page/ebook\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003eLink\u003c/a\u003e to our free Reflex Protect® eBook providing you with important safety tips useful wherever you live, work, worship, or fr-dib\"\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eReflex Protect®, the non-lethal safety preparedness company, successfully lobbied the famously divided Wisconsin legislature to pass Act 52, allowing hospitals and schools to provide Presidia Gel® and other Reflex Protect® anti-violence PPE to frontline personnel. Passed on a bipartisan voice vote and signed by Governor Evers on arrival, the Reflex Protect-sponsored legislation paved the way for passage of Act 97, making caregiver battery a Class H felony later in the session.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eCEO Joe Anderson talked about the importance of protecting caregiver heroes. \"We supported the nurse safety law and offered additional protection to caregivers through anti-violence PPE that can stop an assault before it ever becomes a battery. In addition to protecting healthcare workers, Reflex Protect makes the latest advancements in non-lethal training and product solutions available for first responders, teachers, and all adult Wisconsinites wherever they live, work, worship, and play.”\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eLegislation was required in Wisconsin due to vague and outdated language in its “machine gun law” making the state an outlier in respect of Presidia Gel®. Its prior prohibition is a not an issue for Reflex Protect® in most states. The addition of the Wisconsin market and the public relations catalyzed by passage of Act 52 adds some 4 million individual customers in 2 million homes with accompany government, business, religious, and educational services providing a potential immediate growth opportunity 4X Company's present Montana engagement and adding to increasing growth progress in Idaho, Wyoming, Washington State, Arizona, and Massachusetts\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003ca href=\"https://reflex-protect.ck.page/ebook\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003eLink\u003c/a\u003e to our free Reflex Protect® eBook
